<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Belluzzi" title="Giovanni Battista Belluzzi">Giovanni Battista Belluzzi</a> (1506 in San Marino – 1554) an architect</li>
<li><a href="https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francesco_Maria_Marini" class="extiw" title="it:Francesco Maria Marini">Francesco Maria Marini</a> (di Pesaro) (1630–1686), a composer, playwright, and <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> archbishop</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Antonio_Onofri" title="Antonio Onofri">Antonio Onofri</a> (1759–1825), statesman, "Father of his Country."</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Little_Tony_(singer)" title="Little Tony (singer)">Little Tony</a> (1941 in Tivoli – 2013), a pop and rock musician</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Pasquale_Valentini" title="Pasquale Valentini">Pasquale Valentini</a> (born 1953 in San Marino), a politician who has held multiple ministerial posts</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Massimo_Bonini" title="Massimo Bonini">Massimo Bonini</a> (born 1959 in San Marino), a football player who played for <a href="/wiki/Juventus" class="mw-redirect" title="Juventus">Juventus</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Marco_Macina" title="Marco Macina">Marco Macina</a> (born 1964 in San Marino), a footballer who played for <a href="/wiki/Bologna_FC" class="mw-redirect" title="Bologna FC">Bologna FC</a>, <a href="/wiki/Parma_Calcio_1913" title="Parma Calcio 1913">Parma</a>, <a href="/wiki/Reggio_Audace_F.C." class="mw-redirect" title="Reggio Audace F.C.">Reggiana</a> and <a href="/wiki/AC_Milan" class="mw-redirect" title="AC Milan">AC Milan</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Valentina_Monetta" title="Valentina Monetta">Valentina Monetta</a> (born 1975 in San Marino), a singer who represented San Marino four times in the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision Song Contest</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Manuel_Poggiali" title="Manuel Poggiali">Manuel Poggiali</a> (born 1983 in San Marino), a Grand Prix motorcycle road racing World Champion</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Alex_De_Angelis" class="mw-redirect" title="Alex De Angelis">Alex De Angelis</a> (born 1984 in <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a>), a Grand Prix motorcycle road racer</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Alessandra_Perilli" title="Alessandra Perilli">Alessandra Perilli</a> (born 1988 in <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a>), shooting Olympic silver and bronze medalist and first San Marino citizen to ever win a medal (<a href="/wiki/Tokyo_2020" class="mw-redirect" title="Tokyo 2020">Tokyo 2020</a>)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Gian_Marco_Berti" title="Gian Marco Berti">Gian Marco Berti</a>  (born 1982 in San Marino), shooting Olympic silver medalist and second San Marino citizen to win a medal (<a href="/wiki/Tokyo_2020" class="mw-redirect" title="Tokyo 2020">Tokyo 2020</a>)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Myles_Nazem_Amine" class="mw-redirect" title="Myles Nazem Amine">Myles Nazem Amine</a> (born 1996 in <a href="/wiki/Dearborn,_Michigan" title="Dearborn, Michigan">Dearborn, Michigan</a>), 2020 86&#160;kg wrestling Olympic bronze medalist and third San Marino citizen to win a medal (<a href="/wiki/Tokyo_2020" class="mw-redirect" title="Tokyo 2020">Tokyo 2020</a>)</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_San_Marino" title="Religion in San Marino">Religion in San Marino</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg/220px-Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="168" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg/330px-Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg/440px-Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg 2x" data-file-width="8524" data-file-height="6520" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cathedral_San_Marino_-_Exterior.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/San_Marino_Cathedral" class="mw-redirect" title="San Marino Cathedral">San Marino Cathedral</a></div></div></div>
<p>San Marino is a predominantly <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a> state,<sup id="cite_ref-cia_1-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> though Catholicism is not an <a href="/wiki/Established_religion" class="mw-redirect" title="Established religion">established religion</a>. Over 97% of the population profess the Catholic faith, and approximately half of those regularly attend church.<sup id="cite_ref-Cavendish_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Cavendish-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> There is no <a href="/wiki/Episcopal_see" title="Episcopal see">episcopal see</a> in San Marino, although its name is part of the present diocesan title. Historically, the various parishes in San Marino were divided between two Italian <a href="/wiki/Diocese" title="Diocese">dioceses</a>, mostly in the <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Montefeltro" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Montefeltro">Diocese of Montefeltro</a>, and partly in the <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Rimini" class="mw-redirect" title="Diocese of Rimini">Diocese of Rimini</a>. In 1977, the border between <a href="/wiki/Montefeltro" title="Montefeltro">Montefeltro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a> was readjusted so that all of San Marino fell within the diocese of Montefeltro. The <a href="/wiki/Bishop_of_Montefeltro-San_Marino" class="mw-redirect" title="Bishop of Montefeltro-San Marino">bishop of Montefeltro-San Marino</a> resides in <a href="/wiki/Pennabilli" title="Pennabilli">Pennabilli</a>, in Italy's province of <a href="/wiki/Pesaro_e_Urbino" class="mw-redirect" title="Pesaro e Urbino">Pesaro e Urbino</a>. The country's high <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholic</a> majority can mainly be brought back to the country's founding, where <a href="/wiki/Saint_Marinus" title="Saint Marinus">Saint Marinus</a> set up the first fortress to protect Christians from Roman persecution.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> The small state's culture has primarily remained Catholic ever since.
</p><p>There is a provision under the income tax rules that taxpayers have the right to request the allocation of 0.3% of their income tax to the Catholic Church or to charities.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_San_Marino-Montefeltro" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of San Marino-Montefeltro">Diocese of San Marino-Montefeltro</a> was until 1977 the historic diocese of Montefeltro. It is a <a href="/wiki/Suffragan_diocese" title="Suffragan diocese">suffragan</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Metropolis_(religious_jurisdiction)" title="Metropolis (religious jurisdiction)">metropolitan</a> <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Ravenna-Cervia" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of Ravenna-Cervia">Archdiocese of Ravenna-Cervia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The diocese includes all the <a href="/wiki/Parish" title="Parish">parishes</a> of San Marino. The earliest mention of <a href="/wiki/Montefeltro" title="Montefeltro">Montefeltro</a>, as <i>Mona Feretri</i>, is in the diplomas by which <a href="/wiki/Charlemagne" title="Charlemagne">Charlemagne</a> confirmed the <a href="/wiki/Donation_of_Pepin" title="Donation of Pepin">donation of Pepin</a>. The first known bishop of Montefeltro was Agatho (826), whose residence was at <a href="/wiki/San_Leo" title="San Leo">San Leo</a>. Under Bishop Flaminios Dondi (1724) the see was again transferred to San Leo, but later it returned to Pennabilli. The historic diocese was a suffragan of the <a href="/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Urbino" class="mw-redirect" title="Archdiocese of Urbino">archdiocese of Urbino</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-newadvent.org_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-newadvent.org-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Since 1988, there is formally an <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_nunciature" title="Apostolic nunciature">apostolic nunciature</a> to the republic, but it is vested in the nuncio to Italy.
</p><p>Other faiths include the <a href="/wiki/Waldensians" title="Waldensians">Waldensian Church</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a>.
There has been a <a href="/wiki/Jew" class="mw-redirect" title="Jew">Jewish</a> presence in San Marino for at least 600 years.<sup id="cite_ref-JewishEncyclopedia.com_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-JewishEncyclopedia.com-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The first mention of Jews in San Marino dates to the late 14th century, in official documents recording the business transactions of Jews. There are many documents throughout the 15th to 17th centuries describing Jewish dealings and verifying the presence of a Jewish community in San Marino.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Jews were permitted official protection by the government.
</p><p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, San Marino provided a haven for more than 100,000 Jews and other Italians (approximately 10 times the Sammarinese population at the time) from <a href="/wiki/Nazism" title="Nazism">Nazi</a> persecution. As of 2012<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, few Jews remain.<sup id="cite_ref-jewishvirtuallibrary.org_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jewishvirtuallibrary.org-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> In 2019, a building was inaugurated as the 'Chapel of three religions', the first-ever building of its kind devoted to interfaith dialogue.<sup id="cite_ref-AJB_Video_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-AJB_Video-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_San_Marino" title="Transport in San Marino">Transport in San Marino</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>There are 220&#160;km (140&#160;mi) of roads in the country, the main road being the <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_Highway" title="San Marino Highway">San Marino Highway</a>. Authorities license private vehicles with distinctive <a href="/wiki/Vehicle_registration_plates_of_San_Marino" title="Vehicle registration plates of San Marino">Sammarinese license plates</a>, which are white with blue figures and the coat of arms, usually a letter followed by up to four numbers. Many vehicles also carry the international vehicle identification code (in black on a white oval sticker), which is "RSM".
</p><p>There are no public airports in San Marino, but there is a small private <a href="/wiki/Torraccia_airfield" title="Torraccia airfield">airstrip located in Torraccia</a>. Most tourists who arrive by air land at <a href="/wiki/Federico_Fellini_International_Airport" title="Federico Fellini International Airport">Federico Fellini International Airport</a> (close to the Italian city of <a href="/wiki/Rimini" title="Rimini">Rimini</a>), then make the transfer by bus.
</p><p>Two rivers flow through San Marino, but there is no major water transport, and no port or harbour.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_transport">Public transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Public transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>San Marino public transport comprises eight local bus lines entirely within San Marino. Cross-border connection to Rimini is available across a highway bridge in Rovereta.
</p><p>There is a regular bus service between Rimini and the city of San Marino that is popular with both tourists and workers commuting to San Marino from Italy. This service stops at approximately 20 locations in Rimini and within San Marino, with its two terminus stops at <a href="/wiki/Rimini_railway_station" title="Rimini railway station">Rimini railway station</a> and San Marino coach station.
</p><p>A limited licensed taxi service operates nationwide. There are seven licensed taxi companies operating in the republic,<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> and Italian taxis regularly operate within San Marino when carrying passengers picked up in Italian territory.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg/220px-Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg/330px-Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg/440px-Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3588" data-file-height="2578" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Funivia_di_San_Marino_-_2019-05-07.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Aerial tramway to <a href="/wiki/Monte_Titano" title="Monte Titano">Monte Titano</a></div></div></div>
<p>There is a 1.5-kilometre (1&#160;mi) aerial tramway connecting the <a href="/wiki/City_of_San_Marino" title="City of San Marino">City of San Marino</a> on top of <a href="/wiki/Monte_Titano" title="Monte Titano">Monte Titano</a> with <a href="/wiki/Borgo_Maggiore" title="Borgo Maggiore">Borgo Maggiore</a>, a major town in the republic, with the second largest population of any Sammarinese settlement. From here a further connection is available to the nation's largest settlement, <a href="/wiki/Dogana,_San_Marino" class="mw-redirect" title="Dogana, San Marino">Dogana</a>, via the local bus service.
</p><p>Two aerial tramway cars (gondolas) operate, with service provided at roughly 15-minute intervals throughout the day. A third vehicle is available on the system, a service car for the use of engineers maintaining the tramway.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Railway">Railway</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Railway">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Ferrovia_Rimini%E2%80%93San_Marino" title="Ferrovia Rimini–San Marino">Ferrovia Rimini–San Marino</a></div>
<p>Today, there is no railway in San Marino, but for a short period before <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, it had a single narrow-gauge line called the <a href="/wiki/Ferrovia_Rimini%E2%80%93San_Marino" title="Ferrovia Rimini–San Marino">Ferrovia Rimini–San Marino</a> which connected the country with the Italian rail network at Rimini. Because of the difficulties in accessing the capital, City of San Marino, with its mountain-top location, the terminus station was planned to be located in the village of <a href="/wiki/Valdragone" title="Valdragone">Valdragone</a>, but was extended to reach the capital through a steep and winding track comprising many tunnels. The railway was opened on 12 June 1932.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
An advanced system for its time, it was an electric railway, powered from overhead cables. It was well built and had a high frequency of passengers, but was almost completely destroyed during World War II. Many facilities such as bridges, tunnels, and stations remain visible today, and some have been converted to parks, public footpaths, or traffic routes. Debate continues on whether the line should be restored, as Rimini is the nearest bigger city and has access to the Adriatic sea, the Italian railway network, and the <a href="/wiki/Federico_Fellini_International_Airport" title="Federico Fellini International Airport">Federico Fellini International Airport</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. <div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG/200px-Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG" decoding="async" width="200" height="274" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG/300px-Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG/400px-Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1488" data-file-height="2037" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pompeo_batoni_san_marino_risolleva_la_repubblica_by_stefano_bolognini.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A painting in the Museo di Stato di San Marino by <a href="/wiki/Pompeo_Batoni" title="Pompeo Batoni">Pompeo Batoni</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Three_Towers_of_San_Marino" title="Three Towers of San Marino">Three Towers of San Marino</a> are located on the three peaks of <a href="/wiki/Monte_Titano" title="Monte Titano">Monte Titano</a> in the capital. They are depicted on both the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_San_Marino" title="Flag of San Marino">flag of San Marino</a> and its <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_Arms_of_San_Marino" class="mw-redirect" title="Coat of Arms of San Marino">coat of arms</a>. The three towers are: <i><a href="/wiki/Guaita" title="Guaita">Guaita</a></i>, the oldest of the three (it was constructed in the 11th century); the 13th-century <i><a href="/wiki/Cesta" class="mw-redirect" title="Cesta">Cesta</a></i>, located on the highest of Monte Titano's summits; and the 14th-century <i><a href="/wiki/Montale_(San_Marino)" title="Montale (San Marino)">Montale</a></i>, on the smallest of Monte Titano's summits, still privately owned.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="University">University</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: University">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <span title="Italian-language text"><i lang="it">Università degli Studi della Repubblica di San Marino</i></span> (<a href="/wiki/University_of_the_Republic_of_San_Marino" title="University of the Republic of San Marino">University of the Republic of San Marino</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> is the main university, which includes the <i><a href="/wiki/Scuola_Superiore_di_Studi_Storici_di_San_Marino" title="Scuola Superiore di Studi Storici di San Marino">Scuola Superiore di Studi Storici di San Marino</a></i> (Graduate School of Historical Studies), a distinguished research and advanced international study centre governed by an international Scientific Committee coordinated by the emeritus historian <a href="/wiki/Luciano_Canfora" title="Luciano Canfora">Luciano Canfora</a>. Other important institutes are the <span title="Italian-language text"><i lang="it">Istituto Musicale Sammarinese</i></span> (Sammarinese Musical Institute)<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Akademio_Internacia_de_la_Sciencoj_San_Marino" class="mw-redirect" title="Akademio Internacia de la Sciencoj San Marino">Akademio Internacia de la Sciencoj San Marino</a> or <span title="Italian-language text"><i lang="it">Accademia Internazionale delle Scienze San Marino</i></span> (International Academy of Sciences San Marino).<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The latter is known for adopting <a href="/wiki/Esperanto_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Esperanto language">Esperanto</a> as the language for teaching and for scientific publications; further, it makes wide use of electronic <a href="/wiki/Educational_technology" title="Educational technology">educational technology</a> (also called e-learning).
</p><p>Italian author <a href="/wiki/Umberto_Eco" title="Umberto Eco">Umberto Eco</a> had attempted to create a "university without physical structures" in San Marino.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_San_Marino" title="Sport in San Marino">Sport in San Marino</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_at_the_Olympics" title="San Marino at the Olympics">San Marino at the Olympics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Football_in_San_Marino" title="Football in San Marino">Football in San Marino</a></div>
<p>In San Marino <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a> is the most popular sport. <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">Basketball</a> and <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a> are also popular. The three sports have their own federations, the <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_Football_Federation" title="San Marino Football Federation">San Marino Football Federation</a>, the <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_Basketball_Federation" title="San Marino Basketball Federation">San Marino Basketball Federation</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Federazione_Sammarinese_Pallavolo" class="mw-redirect" title="Federazione Sammarinese Pallavolo">San Marino Volleyball Federation</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_national_football_team" title="San Marino national football team">San Marino national football team</a> has had little success, being made up of part-timers, such as local plumbers and farmers. Never qualifying for a major tournament, and recording only one win in over 25 years of its history, a 1–0 victory in 2004 against <a href="/wiki/Liechtenstein_national_football_team" title="Liechtenstein national football team">Liechtenstein</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> They have drawn four more, with their most notable result being a 1993 0–0 draw with <a href="/wiki/Turkey_national_football_team" title="Turkey national football team">Turkey</a> during the <a href="/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_(UEFA)" title="1994 FIFA World Cup qualification (UEFA)">European qualifiers for the 1994 FIFA World Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> In the same qualifying competition <a href="/wiki/Davide_Gualtieri" title="Davide Gualtieri">Davide Gualtieri</a> scored a goal 8.3 seconds into a match against <a href="/wiki/England_national_football_team" title="England national football team">England</a>; this goal held the record for the fastest in international football until 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> San Marino has a club in the Italian league system called <a href="/wiki/A.S.D.V._San_Marino" class="mw-redirect" title="A.S.D.V. San Marino">A.S.D.V. San Marino</a> and a domestic amateur league, the <a href="/wiki/Campionato_Sammarinese" class="mw-redirect" title="Campionato Sammarinese">Campionato Sammarinese</a>, whose teams also participate in European club competitions. Together with Italy, San Marino held the <a href="/wiki/2019_UEFA_European_Under-21_Championship" title="2019 UEFA European Under-21 Championship">2019 UEFA European Under-21 Championship</a>, with teams playing at the <a href="/wiki/Stadio_Olimpico_(San_Marino)" class="mw-redirect" title="Stadio Olimpico (San Marino)">Stadio Olimpico</a> in <a href="/wiki/Serravalle_(San_Marino)" title="Serravalle (San Marino)">Serravalle</a>. With Italy being the sole automatic qualifiers, the Sammarinese team was not participating in the final tournament.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg/220px-GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg/330px-GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg/440px-GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:GP_Imola2005_SchumiAlonso.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>2005 <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_Grand_Prix" title="San Marino Grand Prix">San Marino Grand Prix</a> held in <a href="/wiki/Imola" title="Imola">Imola</a>, Italy</div></div></div>
<p>A <a href="/wiki/Formula_One" title="Formula One">Formula One</a> race, the <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_Grand_Prix" title="San Marino Grand Prix">San Marino Grand Prix</a>, was named after the state, although it did not take place there. Instead, it was held at the <a href="/wiki/Autodromo_Enzo_e_Dino_Ferrari" class="mw-redirect" title="Autodromo Enzo e Dino Ferrari">Autodromo Enzo e Dino Ferrari</a> in the Italian town of <a href="/wiki/Imola" title="Imola">Imola</a>, about 100&#160;km (60&#160;mi) northwest of San Marino. <a href="/wiki/Roland_Ratzenberger" title="Roland Ratzenberger">Roland Ratzenberger</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ayrton_Senna" title="Ayrton Senna">Ayrton Senna</a> suffered fatal accidents a day apart during the <a href="/wiki/1994_San_Marino_Grand_Prix" title="1994 San Marino Grand Prix">1994 Grand Prix</a>. This international event was removed from the calendar in <a href="/wiki/2007_Formula_One_season" class="mw-redirect" title="2007 Formula One season">2007</a>, although the circuit has since returned to the calendar as the <a href="/wiki/Emilia-Romagna_Grand_Prix" class="mw-redirect" title="Emilia-Romagna Grand Prix">Emilia-Romagna Grand Prix</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_and_Rimini%27s_Coast_motorcycle_Grand_Prix" class="mw-redirect" title="San Marino and Rimini&#39;s Coast motorcycle Grand Prix">San Marino and Rimini's Coast motorcycle Grand Prix</a> was reinstated in the schedule in 2007 and takes place at the <a href="/wiki/Misano_World_Circuit_Marco_Simoncelli" title="Misano World Circuit Marco Simoncelli">Misano World Circuit Marco Simoncelli</a>, as does San Marino's round of the <a href="/wiki/World_Superbike_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="World Superbike Championship">World Superbike Championship</a>.
</p><p>San Marino has <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_(baseball_team)" class="mw-redirect" title="San Marino (baseball team)">a professional baseball team</a> which plays in <a href="/wiki/Italian_Baseball_League" title="Italian Baseball League">Italy's top division</a>. It has participated in the <a href="/wiki/European_Cup_(baseball)" title="European Cup (baseball)">European Cup</a> tournament for the continent's top club sides several times, hosting the event in 1996, 2000, 2004, and <a href="/wiki/2007_European_Cup_(baseball)" title="2007 European Cup (baseball)">2007</a>. It won the championship in <a href="/w/index.php?title=2006_Final_Four_(baseball)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="2006 Final Four (baseball) (page does not exist)">2006</a>, <a href="/wiki/2011_Final_Four_(baseball)" title="2011 Final Four (baseball)">2011</a> and <a href="/w/index.php?title=2014_Final_Four_(baseball)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="2014 Final Four (baseball) (page does not exist)">2014</a>.
</p><p>Shooting is also very popular in San Marino, with many shooters having taken part in international competitions and the <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympic Games</a>. San Marino is also the smallest country to have ever won an <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_at_the_Olympics" title="San Marino at the Olympics">Olympic medal</a>, having won one silver and a bronze in shooting in the <a href="/wiki/2020_Summer_Olympics" title="2020 Summer Olympics">2020 Summer Olympics</a> in Tokyo. Despite their small population, they got their silver in a team event losing the gold medal match to Spain 41–40.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sammarinese_cuisine" title="Sammarinese cuisine">Sammarinese cuisine</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Piadina.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Piadina.jpg/200px-Piadina.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="197" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Piadina.jpg/300px-Piadina.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Piadina.jpg/400px-Piadina.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2088" data-file-height="2052" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Piadina.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <i><a href="/wiki/Piadina" title="Piadina">piadina</a></i>, a dish characteristic of the Italian region of Romagna and of its enclave of San Marino</div></div></div>
<p>The cuisine of San Marino is extremely similar to <a href="/wiki/Italian_cuisine" title="Italian cuisine">Italian</a>, especially that of the adjoining <a href="/wiki/Emilia-Romagna" title="Emilia-Romagna">Emilia-Romagna</a> and <a href="/wiki/Marche" title="Marche">Marche</a> regions, but it has a number of its own unique dishes and products.<sup id="cite_ref-Marshall_Cavendish_2010_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Marshall_Cavendish_2010-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Its best known is probably the <i><a href="/wiki/Torta_Tre_Monti" title="Torta Tre Monti">Torta Tre Monti</a></i> ("Cake of the Three Mountains" or "Cake of the Three Towers"), a wafer layered cake covered in chocolate depicting the <a href="/wiki/Three_Towers_of_San_Marino" title="Three Towers of San Marino">Three Towers of San Marino</a>. The country also has a small <a href="/wiki/Sammarinese_wine" title="Sammarinese wine">wine</a> industry.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="UNESCO">UNESCO</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: UNESCO">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The site San Marino: Historic Centre and Mount Titano became part of the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> World Heritage List in 2008. The decision was taken during the 32nd Session of the UNESCO <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Committee" title="World Heritage Committee">World Heritage Committee</a> composed of 21 Countries convened in Québec, Canada.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_San_Marino" title="Music of San Marino">Music of San Marino</a> and <a href="/wiki/San_Marino_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="San Marino in the Eurovision Song Contest">San Marino in the Eurovision Song Contest</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">August 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The country has a long and rich musical tradition, closely linked to that of Italy, but which is also highly independent in itself. A well-known 17th-century composer is Francesco Maria Marini. The pop singer <a href="/wiki/Little_Tony_(singer)" title="Little Tony (singer)">Little Tony</a> achieved considerable success in the United Kingdom and Italy in the 1950s and 1960s.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/San_Marino_in_the_Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="San Marino in the Eurovision Song Contest">San Marino</a> has taken part in the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest" title="Eurovision Song Contest">Eurovision Song Contest</a> eleven times, achieving three final qualifications to date (with then-three, eventually four-time contestant and San Marino native <a href="/wiki/Valentina_Monetta" title="Valentina Monetta">Valentina Monetta</a> with "<a href="/wiki/Maybe_(Valentina_Monetta_song)" class="mw-redirect" title="Maybe (Valentina Monetta song)">Maybe</a>" in <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2014" title="Eurovision Song Contest 2014">2014</a>, Turkish singer <a href="/wiki/Serhat_(singer)" title="Serhat (singer)">Serhat</a> with "<a href="/wiki/Say_Na_Na_Na" title="Say Na Na Na">Say Na Na Na</a>" who achieved 19th place in the final in <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2019" title="Eurovision Song Contest 2019">2019</a> and Italian singer <a href="/wiki/Senhit_(singer)" title="Senhit (singer)">Senhit</a> along with American rapper <a href="/wiki/Flo_Rida" title="Flo Rida">Flo Rida</a> who qualified for the <a href="/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2021" title="Eurovision Song Contest 2021">2021</a> final with the song "<a href="/wiki/Adrenalina_(Senhit_song)" title="Adrenalina (Senhit song)">Adrenalina</a>").
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_holidays_and_festivals">Public holidays and festivals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=San_Marino&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Public holidays and festivals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<li id="cite_note-43"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-43">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.ecomuseorimini.it/parco-ausa/">"Parco Ausa"</a>. Ecomuseo Rimini<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">14 March</span> 2022</span>. <q>Nel corso della metà del secolo scorso "l'Ausa, che si stava trasformando sempre di più in una fogna", è stato deviato e fatto confluire nelle acque del Marecchia per mezzo di sponde cementificate che, dai pressi del casello autostradale di Rimini Sud, costeggiano la collina di Covignano. A partire dagli anni sessanta il vecchio corso che sfociava in mare all'altezza di Piazzale Kennedy, venne tombificato.</q> &#91;During the middle of the last century, "the Ausa, which was increasingly turning into a sewer", was diverted and made to flow into the waters of the Marecchia by means of cemented banks which, near the Rimini Sud motorway exit, skirt the Covignano hill. Starting from the sixties, the old course that flowed into the sea at Piazzale Kennedy was buried.&#93;</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Parco+Ausa&amp;rft.pub=Ecomuseo+Rimini&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.ecomuseorimini.it%2Fparco-ausa%2F&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASan+Marino" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
